# ovis-terminal-v1 - Work Plan

## TL;DR (For humans)

**Who this is for and what changes for them:** Coding agents and the Ovis phone/browser client get a terminal runtime that answers them with structured, typed facts instead of screen text and guesses: a persistent connection that never drops the second request, operations that land on the exact terminal they name, agent status that stays what the agent declared, prompts and attach outcomes as records, and terminals that outlive a daemon restart. A person at the keyboard keeps control through explicit leases: observers and automation can watch and type only when granted, never by stealing focus.

**What you'll get:** A new public repository devswha/ovis-terminal with a Rust runtime (daemon, dumb PTY holder that survives daemon restarts, VT screen engine, SQLite state), a documented machine-readable API (OpenRPC schema, generated TypeScript client, replayable conformance fixtures), an agent CLI with an embedded manual, remote machines over SSH, release artifacts for Linux and macOS (Windows built and smoke-tested in CI), and a migration guide that maps every Ovis workaround to the contract that replaces it. Every change arrives as its own pull request whose body shows the herdr/hc behavior before and the ovis-terminal command output after. Implementation complete means all todos 1-70 and F1-F6 pass with Todo 70's continuous 30-minute soak proof. Release complete means the scheduled continuous 72-hour soak results for Linux and macOS have landed and the release evidence gates pass.

**Why this approach:** herdr's TUI, brand detection, focus-coupled targets and one-request sockets are what Ovis fights today (5,174 catalogued violations, 5 live-reproduced traps), while herdr-core's headless design (framed wire protocol, OSC 7501 status, parent/child agents, typed errors with hints) is worth keeping; the plan keeps the second set and excludes the first by contract, and Rust was chosen at the approval gate for a small static binary and the hc precedent.

**What it will NOT do:** No terminal UI of its own (Ovis is the UI), no agent-brand detection or screen-text status guessing, no edits to the Ovis repository (a later Ovis PR adopts the published contract), no MVP subset of the ideal state, and no 72-hour soak claimed inside this run (the soak harness ships and its scheduled run is reported when it lands).

**Effort:** XL
**Risk:** High - a greenfield multi-process runtime with PTY, persistence and protocol work on three operating systems, mitigated by native platform probes in Todo 12 (wave 3), a conformance runner that drives the real socket, and per-todo Before/After evidence.
**Decisions to sanity-check:** Rust over Bun/TypeScript (user decision at the gate; the host has no Rust toolchain, so an isolated rustup lives under the run root and CI carries the official toolchains); MIT license with Apache-2.0 attribution for herdr/herdr-core-derived material; Linux and macOS first-class with Windows contract-complete and CI-smoke-only; shared PTY holder per build with core-owned checkpoints; alacritty_terminal as the VT engine; the 72-hour soak deferred to a scheduled workflow.

Your next move: the plan is being reviewed in adversarial rounds (verdict recorded in the draft); execution follows the resolved review via ulw-execute with one PR per lane. Full execution detail follows below.

---

> TL;DR (machine): XL effort, high risk; 70 implementation todos in 20 waves + 6 final verifiers; delivers a Rust agent-native terminal runtime, OpenRPC/TS client contract, CLI, remote machines, releases and migration guide for devswha/ovis-terminal.

## Scope

### Affected user and ideal state

**Coding agents.** Today agents can receive truthful answers for the wrong focused pane, pay a five-second send delay, and miss output produced before a wait is registered. The runtime gives them explicit stable targets, typed errors, durable operation receipts, cursor-based waits, and authoritative declared agent records without opening a TUI.

**Ovis client.** Today the Bun + React client opens one socket per RPC and compensates for subscription gaps, attach diagnostics, platform differences, prompt parsing, and transcript formats. It will consume one persistent, versioned, authenticated client contract with atomic snapshots, replay, structured attach/input, prompts, and conversation records; this plan ships OpenRPC, generated TypeScript, and executable fixtures in this repository, with Ovis adoption left to a separate PR.

**Human at the terminal.** Today observation, focus, takeover, history reads, and browser geometry can disturb the operator's application or erase completion state. Explicit independent input and geometry leases, non-mutating reads, stable layout IDs, and authorized takeover preserve human control while multiple observers and agents use the same terminal.

**Remote machines.** Today remote runtimes have differing lifecycle and capability contracts, and clients must infer identity, recovery, and Windows behavior. Each machine will expose authenticated identity, bounded independent work queues, transparent transport, durable recovery evidence, and logout-independent PTY ownership; lost links remain visible and never redirect work locally.

The following 117 statements are copied from `docs/design/ideal-state.md`; IS-<n> is exactly IDEAL-<n>, not a reduced MVP. The reason column is copied from the same binding ledger.

| Row | Statement | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| IS-001 = IDEAL-001 | Endpoint discovery returns a connectable address, transport kind, and environment override on Linux, macOS, and Windows without treating a marker file as a live socket. | Ovis must connect without platform-specific path guessing. |
| IS-002 = IDEAL-002 | The handshake returns authenticated machine identity, runtime lifetime, control-server boot ID, protocol generation, methods, capabilities, and negotiated limits. | A bridge restart must not masquerade as a new terminal lifetime. |
| IS-003 = IDEAL-003 | An unsupported protocol generation produces `unsupported_generation` before any state-changing request can run. | Incompatible peers must fail at a defined boundary. |
| IS-004 = IDEAL-004 | One established connection accepts concurrent correlated RPCs, subscription changes, and terminal channels until an explicit close or transport failure. | Reusing a connection must not discard later requests. |
| IS-005 = IDEAL-005 | Every accepted RPC request has a unique string ID and receives exactly one terminal result or structured error with that same ID while the connection remains writable. | Interleaved traffic must not be mistaken for another call's answer. |
| IS-006 = IDEAL-006 | Incremental frame decoding produces identical records for every fragmentation or coalescing of valid input bytes, with JSON control and terminal bytes on distinct channels. | Socket read boundaries cannot define application messages. |
| IS-007 = IDEAL-007 | A negotiated frame is at most 16 MiB, and paginated or chunked reads can return at least 64 MiB logical results with explicit total, continuation, and truncation fields. | Large reads need bounded transport without silent data loss. |
| IS-008 = IDEAL-008 | Every malformed method or parameter, including an unknown targeting field, is rejected before dispatch with a stable kind and offending field path. | Ignored arguments must never select the focused pane. |
| IS-009 = IDEAL-009 | Every target-sensitive call requires a typed resource ID or an explicitly requested caller binding, and ambiguous names return structured candidates without acting. | Automation must never inherit an unrelated human selection. |
| IS-010 = IDEAL-010 | Every RPC has a caller-visible deadline, defaulting to 10 seconds, and long-running work returns a queryable operation ID before that deadline. | Worktree and remote operations must not hold an ordinary call indefinitely. |
| IS-011 = IDEAL-011 | Cancellation yields `cancelled_before_commit`, `already_committed` with a receipt, or a queryable pending outcome and never claims to undo committed effects. | Disconnect and cancellation must not invite unsafe retries. |
| IS-012 = IDEAL-012 | Mutations accept an operation ID whose result is retained for the advertised deduplication window, and an expired ID cannot be silently replayed as new work. | A lost reply must not duplicate input or resource creation. |
| IS-013 = IDEAL-013 | Errors contain a stable kind, message, recovery hint, retryability, target identity when known, and an operation ID whenever effects may have begun. | Clients need machine-readable recovery rather than prose classification. |
| IS-014 = IDEAL-014 | Health and status queries return running, stopped, unreachable, or stale-endpoint state without starting, installing, or restarting a runtime. | Observing a stopped service must leave it stopped. |
| IS-015 = IDEAL-015 | Subscription startup returns a subscription ID and snapshot at revision R, followed only by matching changes committed after R. | Initial state and subsequent events must have no blind interval. |
| IS-016 = IDEAL-016 | Every event has one envelope containing schema version, machine and runtime identity, sequence, type, resource IDs, and typed data. | Status and lifecycle events must not require unrelated parsers. |
| IS-017 = IDEAL-017 | State snapshots and events share a monotonic sequence domain per runtime lifetime, and stale snapshot revisions are explicitly distinguishable. | A delayed snapshot must not overwrite newer state. |
| IS-018 = IDEAL-018 | Adding, replacing, or removing a subscription filter is acknowledged with an effective sequence and preserves all unaffected subscriptions. | A second subscribe must not kill the first stream. |
| IS-019 = IDEAL-019 | Reconnect with a retained cursor replays subsequent matching events, otherwise returning `missed` with a fresh snapshot and its sequence. | Consumers need deterministic recovery after disconnection. |
| IS-020 = IDEAL-020 | Each subscription emits a heartbeat within 15 seconds of idle time on every supported transport, carrying its current sequence and runtime identity. | An idle terminal must be distinguishable from a dead event path. |
| IS-021 = IDEAL-021 | Slow subscriptions have advertised queue bounds and receive loss or close state recoverable on reconnect even when the final error frame could not be delivered. | A full socket cannot be trusted to carry its own last diagnostic. |
| IS-022 = IDEAL-022 | Filtering exposes an acknowledged scan cursor, so sequence gaps caused by nonmatching events are distinguishable from delivery loss. | A global event counter alone cannot prove loss in a filtered stream. |
| IS-023 = IDEAL-023 | A complete snapshot includes workspaces, tabs, panes, layout, agents, sessions, terminal generations, geometry, and restoration state at one revision. | Ovis must not assemble an apparently atomic roster from unrelated reads. |
| IS-024 = IDEAL-024 | Every committed create, close, move, layout change, and agent binding change emits a revisioned event naming all affected resources. | A mutation must not require clients to invent missing lifecycle notifications. |
| IS-025 = IDEAL-025 | Completion events carry agent/session identity, turn ID, finish generation, and outcome, with the same values recoverable from snapshots. | Replayed or delayed completions must be deduplicable. |
| IS-026 = IDEAL-026 | A newly registered state wait first evaluates current state and then subscribes atomically, returning immediately when already satisfied. | Already-idle or already-exited resources must not cause lost wakeups. |
| IS-027 = IDEAL-027 | Under the declared local service profile, committed status and lifecycle changes reach a writable subscribed connection within 100 ms without polling. | Fast status delivery must be a measured contract rather than an incidental timer result. |
| IS-028 = IDEAL-028 | Attach returns a structured ready, held, refused, recovering, or ended result, including terminal generation, stream ID, geometry, and reason. | Attach control state must not be inferred from terminal text. |
| IS-029 = IDEAL-029 | Multiple observers attach concurrently without taking input ownership, changing the grid, or displacing an existing viewer. | Watching from a phone must not disturb the operator. |
| IS-030 = IDEAL-030 | A controller lease identifies its owner and generation, and competing acquisition returns a conflict naming that owner without eviction. | Input ownership must be explicit and inspectable. |
| IS-031 = IDEAL-031 | Takeover requires an authorized explicit action against the current lease generation and emits revoked/acquired events naming both owners. | A bridge reconnect must not silently seize the terminal. |
| IS-032 = IDEAL-032 | A late attach starts with a coherent full screen, cursor, terminal modes, grid, history cursor, and output offset before live bytes after that offset. | A tail of terminal diffs cannot reconstruct content painted only once. |
| IS-033 = IDEAL-033 | Attach channels carry only terminal data, while refusal, handoff, takeover, loss, and exit travel as typed control records. | Setup noise must never become displayed output or returned input. |
| IS-034 = IDEAL-034 | Every supported operating system provides direct ordered byte-stream attach, cursor and mode state, resize, and output acknowledgement semantics. | Windows mirroring must not remain a lower-fidelity terminal contract. |
| IS-035 = IDEAL-035 | Screen, history, search, and selection reads neither inject input nor change the application's viewport, and do not refuse an otherwise valid attach. | Inspection must not scroll or pause a live application. |
| IS-036 = IDEAL-036 | Every terminal read returns source, format, content revision, requested and returned range, truncation, completeness, and a reason for unavailable history. | A screen snapshot must not be represented as complete application history. |
| IS-037 = IDEAL-037 | Geometry has a controller lease separate from observation, with explicit release and deterministic transfer among eligible interact clients. | A hidden browser viewport must not keep resizing the operator's pane. |
| IS-038 = IDEAL-038 | A geometry change record precedes output at the new grid, and redraw completion identifies that grid and its revision before input-ready is asserted. | Stale-size first paint must not be accepted as current readiness. |
| IS-039 = IDEAL-039 | Explicit repaint returns a coherent screen at the unchanged grid without fake shrink/restore operations or injected application keys. | Recovery must not perturb terminal dimensions to obtain a picture. |
| IS-040 = IDEAL-040 | Each output subscriber has its own stream identity, byte offset, credit, and bounded backlog; stale or impossible acknowledgements cannot grant credit. | One attachment's acknowledgements must not affect another stream. |
| IS-041 = IDEAL-041 | A stalled viewer is resynchronized with an explicit gap and checkpoint or disconnected, without pausing other viewers or suppressing control input. | Slow observation must not freeze an operator's terminal. |
| IS-042 = IDEAL-042 | Launch accepts explicit executable, argv, cwd, environment, rows, and columns, and reports spawn failure separately from an admitted launch operation. | Running a program must not depend on typing a shell command into a detected prompt. |
| IS-043 = IDEAL-043 | Terminal bytes preserve order and UTF-8 sequences across read boundaries, and the final output offset is delivered before process-exit completion. | Final output must not disappear when the child exits quickly. |
| IS-044 = IDEAL-044 | Input buffering is bounded and separate from resize, cancel, and stop control queues, with overflow rejected before accepting more bytes. | A program that does not read stdin must remain controllable. |
| IS-045 = IDEAL-045 | Pane ID, terminal incarnation, process incarnation, and attach-stream ID are distinct fields, and handoff explicitly states which survived. | A new stream or terminal must not inherit readiness from an old one. |
| IS-046 = IDEAL-046 | Keyboard, mouse, bracketed-paste, alternate-screen, and cursor modes are available as revisioned structured state and drive encoding rather than brand guesses. | Logical input must match the program's negotiated terminal modes. |
| IS-047 = IDEAL-047 | The runtime owns authoritative scrollback and exposes revision-guarded search and selection without reconstructing an attached screen from periodic reads. | Browser history must not diverge from the live terminal stream. |
| IS-048 = IDEAL-048 | Input admission atomically checks machine, runtime, pane, terminal, process/session incarnation, readiness, and caller lease against the same target that receives the write. | A check followed by an unrelated write leaves a replacement-process race. |
| IS-049 = IDEAL-049 | Raw bytes, logical keys, paste, composer submissions, and agent sends enter one per-terminal ordered input sequence with acknowledged barriers. | Mixed input paths must not overtake each other. |
| IS-050 = IDEAL-050 | A submission's text and commit action are atomic relative to other writers, with delivery offsets and partial-write failure reported explicitly. | Another writer must not enter the middle of a prompt submission. |
| IS-051 = IDEAL-051 | Send returns admitted or rejected within 100 ms under the local service profile and never waits for the program to become working. | A plain shell must not incur hc's observed five-second status wait. |
| IS-052 = IDEAL-052 | Receipts distinguish queued, PTY-written, agent-accepted, turn-started, and turn-ended, leaving unsupported stages explicitly unknown. | PTY delivery alone does not prove a program accepted a task. |
| IS-053 = IDEAL-053 | An output wait accepts a previously captured cursor or atomically arms with send, matches retained output since that boundary, and reports an expired cursor as a gap. | Output printed during send must not be lost to a later wait. |
| IS-054 = IDEAL-054 | Interrupt returns the action delivered and observed process/turn outcome separately, and reports stopped only after matching process-exit or explicit agent-stop evidence. | Sending Escape must not claim that a still-running process stopped. |
| IS-055 = IDEAL-055 | Disconnect revokes input-ready, and reconnect never automatically resubmits input whose delivery is uncertain. | Replaying typed commands can duplicate effects after a lost reply. |
| IS-056 = IDEAL-056 | Blocked input is rejected with a typed reason unless a versioned prompt answer or separately authorized explicit raw interaction applies. | Ordinary sends must not accidentally answer an approval dialog. |
| IS-057 = IDEAL-057 | Prompt events contain prompt ID, version, session/process binding, kind, choices or field schema, answer mechanism, and active/closed state. | Ovis must build a prompt card without reading glyphs or footer hints. |
| IS-058 = IDEAL-058 | Prompt answer atomically validates prompt version, liveness, session identity, answer schema, and authority, then returns accepted or a typed conflict without guessed key presses. | A stale card must not answer a different prompt. |
| IS-059 = IDEAL-059 | Open questions are independent records from turn execution state, so a working or completed turn can still have an unanswered question. | Turn status alone cannot represent pending user input. |
| IS-060 = IDEAL-060 | A queued first message is bound to launch ID, requesting principal, and declared readiness, with accepted, sent, cancelled, or expired state queryable. | Startup must not type a task into an initialization dialog. |
| IS-061 = IDEAL-061 | Multiline paste is encoded against the current bracketed-paste mode or rejected before delivery, and ordinary text is never promoted into control input. | A composer insertion must not unexpectedly execute embedded lines or escapes. |
| IS-062 = IDEAL-062 | Agent integrations can submit and answer typed in-process prompts without providing a terminal-key equivalent. | A widget that accepts only extension events must remain usable from Ovis. |
| IS-063 = IDEAL-063 | Turn waits bind to a specific send or turn ID and return completed, blocked, exited, lost, cancelled, or timeout without treating timeout as completion. | Another turn or a background child must not satisfy the wrong wait. |
| IS-064 = IDEAL-064 | Any authenticated program can register an arbitrary agent label and session reference without membership in a built-in brand list. | New agents must have the same identity contract as known brands. |
| IS-065 = IDEAL-065 | Agent lookup returns the registered record or explicit `agent: null` for a valid non-agent pane, reserving not-found for missing resources. | A plain shell must not be an exceptional lookup failure. |
| IS-066 = IDEAL-066 | Accepted sequenced status reports remain authoritative until replaced, cleared, or invalidated by their declared lifetime, never by screen or process-name heuristics. | The runtime must not undo a program's report 200 ms later. |
| IS-067 = IDEAL-067 | Status distinguishes working, idle, blocked, done, background, error, and unreported, with origin, session, sequence, and turn identity in both snapshots and events. | Startup and live updates must tell the same story. |
| IS-068 = IDEAL-068 | Stale or conflicting status reports return a typed rejection with current sequence instead of a success acknowledgement that hides non-application. | A producer needs to know whether its report changed state. |
| IS-069 = IDEAL-069 | A turn outcome and finish generation never change merely because a human focused, viewed, or acknowledged the pane. | Completion is a fact independent of notification presentation. |
| IS-070 = IDEAL-070 | Parent/child links, child status records, notification policy, and die-with-parent behavior are explicitly declared and do not relabel the parent's session. | Delegation must not confuse provider-child activity with the orchestrator's turn. |
| IS-071 = IDEAL-071 | Process inspection identifies shell, launcher, foreground and background processes with PID plus start token, or reports unsupported fields explicitly. | Missing foreground evidence must not be mistaken for exit or PID continuity. |
| IS-072 = IDEAL-072 | Session binding includes agent ID, process incarnation, source, reference kind and value, history identity, and revision, changing explicitly on resume or replacement. | A transcript must not be selected by directory or recency alone. |
| IS-073 = IDEAL-073 | Conversation reads and events provide typed user, assistant, tool-call, and tool-result records with stable IDs, ordering, and tool-call linkage. | A common conversation surface must not confuse terminal menus with messages. |
| IS-074 = IDEAL-074 | Message records preserve commentary versus final phase and all mixed text/tool-result content blocks. | Progress and mixed provider records must not be dropped or misrepresented. |
| IS-075 = IDEAL-075 | Conversation pagination cursors bind to history identity and revision, and detect append, truncation, replacement, and same-size rewrites. | Cached history must not silently survive a changed underlying transcript. |
| IS-076 = IDEAL-076 | Branch, fork, rewind, and resume records explicitly select the active history chain while retaining addressable off-branch records. | Old abandoned ranges must not appear as part of the current conversation. |
| IS-077 = IDEAL-077 | Turn records expose start and end boundaries, outcome and available timestamps, while model and reasoning metadata are nullable reported fields. | Idle time and footer guesses must not inflate duration or invent metadata. |
| IS-078 = IDEAL-078 | History listings are bounded, newest-first, and include title, timestamps, agent/session IDs, and typed resume references without following paths outside authorized stores. | History discovery must be safe and independent of provider file layout. |
| IS-079 = IDEAL-079 | Missing structured conversation or prompt support is advertised as unavailable with provenance, and any displayed terminal text remains explicitly terminal output. | An unsupported agent must not produce fabricated chat records. |
| IS-080 = IDEAL-080 | Local and remote resource addresses resolve through stable machine IDs and opaque resource IDs, with mutable display names unable to redirect an existing binding. | Renaming or reconnecting a machine must not change the destination of queued work. |
| IS-081 = IDEAL-081 | Remote transport authenticates the expected runtime, keeps protocol bytes separate from diagnostics, and returns typed authentication or trust failures. | SSH banners and credential prompts must not corrupt the wire stream. |
| IS-082 = IDEAL-082 | A lost remote line marks its resources unavailable with last-known revision and never silently omits them or falls back to a local target. | The roster must distinguish unreachable resources from deleted ones. |
| IS-083 = IDEAL-083 | Each machine has independent deadlines, cancellation, queues, and failure state, so a stalled remote cannot block local work or another machine. | Cross-machine orchestration must remain responsive during partial outages. |
| IS-084 = IDEAL-084 | Pane, tab, workspace, and split-node IDs survive layout moves, and structural mutations preserve each client's selection unless that client explicitly requests a focus change. | Browser selection and automation must not yank a terminal user's focus. |
| IS-085 = IDEAL-085 | Workspace/tab creation returns all created IDs and a separate agent-launch outcome, including resources left behind after partial failure. | A failed agent launch must not hide a successfully created workspace. |
| IS-086 = IDEAL-086 | Split, zoom, swap, resize, and ratio changes require explicit IDs and return resulting geometry, revision, and changed or no-op reason without silent clamping. | Layout controls must have deterministic effects independent of focused panes. |
| IS-087 = IDEAL-087 | Worktree operations accept explicit repository and checkout identities and return branch, source relationship, dirty/locked state, open workspaces, and operation outcome. | Clients must not retry from a guessed source workspace. |
| IS-088 = IDEAL-088 | Close and worktree-remove requests identify all affected resources and require explicit group or force scope before touching resources beyond the named target. | A single-pane action must not unexpectedly close a linked group. |
| IS-089 = IDEAL-089 | Templates are discoverable by stable name and version and launch through the same explicit argv, parent, readiness, and first-message contract as direct programs. | Agents should select roles without embedding private launch conventions. |
| IS-090 = IDEAL-090 | Plugin discovery returns identity, version, enabled state, action schema, applicable contexts, and an explicit unavailability reason for non-invocable actions. | Ovis must not offer actions that the runtime will silently refuse. |
| IS-091 = IDEAL-091 | Plugin invocation returns a durable run ID with queryable state, bounded output, truncation, exit result, and IDs of resources it created. | Printed output must not be the only way to discover a plugin-created pane. |
| IS-092 = IDEAL-092 | Remote orphan notifications are acknowledged and replayed after reconnect, and die-with-parent decisions use parent incarnation rather than a reused address. | Network loss must not abandon declared child-lifecycle obligations. |
| IS-093 = IDEAL-093 | PTY holders continue owning and draining programs through control-server crash, planned restart, client disconnect, and SSH logout on every supported platform. | A frontend or transport lifetime must not become the program's lifetime. |
| IS-094 = IDEAL-094 | Planned restart preserves program incarnations and terminal state, emits recovery boundaries, and returns explicit outcomes for interrupted operations and waits. | Preserved processes must not imply preserved sockets or in-flight replies. |
| IS-095 = IDEAL-095 | Crash recovery reports retained output ranges, any gaps, screen exactness, and new stream identities before restoring readiness. | Recovered tail bytes must not be passed off as complete history. |
| IS-096 = IDEAL-096 | After host reboot, persisted resources are listed as restored metadata or explicitly relaunched processes with new incarnations, never as surviving pre-reboot processes. | Layout persistence is not process survival. |
| IS-097 = IDEAL-097 | A durability acknowledgement is issued only after atomic state commit, and recovery exposes corrupted or unsupported records without silently replacing them with healthy-looking defaults. | Acknowledged state must remain distinguishable from lost state. |
| IS-098 = IDEAL-098 | `doc.get` returns value and version, while `doc.set` atomically compares version, commits a bounded value, and emits a revisioned change or typed conflict. | Shared agent state needs compare-and-swap rather than last-writer-wins races. |
| IS-099 = IDEAL-099 | Server stop, control-server restart, terminal kill, and metadata removal are distinct operations whose receipts enumerate affected process incarnations. | An administrative verb must not hide whether programs were terminated. |
| IS-100 = IDEAL-100 | Updates expose staged verification, activation, rollback outcome, and progress without replacing the active runtime on a failed integrity or compatibility check. | Remote installation failure must leave an inspectable runnable state. |
| IS-101 = IDEAL-101 | Retention limits for history, events, receipts, plugin runs, and finished terminals are advertised, and eviction advances a visible floor or tombstone rather than returning an empty success. | Clients need to distinguish never-existing data from expired data. |
| IS-102 = IDEAL-102 | Every connection has an authenticated principal and resource scope, with local endpoint permissions and remote credential validation enforced before access. | A transport connection alone must not grant unrestricted machine authority. |
| IS-103 = IDEAL-103 | Observe credentials cannot send input, answer prompts, resize, take over, launch, mutate documents, or change workspace state through any API or stream frame. | Read-only behavior must hold beyond the Ovis UI. |
| IS-104 = IDEAL-104 | Credentials are runtime-scoped, revocable, rotatable, and expiring, and revoked authority is rechecked before each mutation including queued writes. | A previously authorized socket must not retain stale write rights. |
| IS-105 = IDEAL-105 | Agent report authority is bound to a registered process/session and cannot overwrite another agent's identity, prompt, status, or conversation. | Explicit reporting still needs an ownership boundary. |
| IS-106 = IDEAL-106 | Prompt-answer and takeover permissions are checked independently of blocked status or possession of a terminal address. | Blocked-state convenience must not substitute for access control. |
| IS-107 = IDEAL-107 | Executable launches, paths, payload sizes, and remote operations are validated against typed schemas and caller scope without shell interpolation of protocol values. | Structured requests must not become ambiguous remote shell commands. |
| IS-108 = IDEAL-108 | Runtime diagnostics and audit records redact credentials and secret-input payloads, and stored terminal/history data obey explicit access and retention policy. | Observability must not create a second unprotected copy of sensitive input. |
| IS-109 = IDEAL-109 | Plugin actions and machine administration require explicit granted scopes and cannot inherit another client's lease or authority. | Extension execution must respect the runtime's access boundary. |
| IS-110 = IDEAL-110 | `--json` produces schema-defined output regardless of TTY state, with success on stdout, structured failure on stderr, and published stable exit codes. | Scripts must not infer output mode from invocation environment. |
| IS-111 = IDEAL-111 | CLI parse errors exit before connection, targetless automation returns `needs_target` with structured candidates, and help, schema, and skill commands do not start a daemon. | Discovery and invalid commands must be safe to run unattended. |
| IS-112 = IDEAL-112 | One versioned machine-readable schema defines requests, replies, events, errors, channels, limits, and capabilities with collision-free type names for generated clients. | Ovis types must not drift from the runtime's actual contract. |
| IS-113 = IDEAL-113 | Same-generation compatibility preserves existing methods and required outputs, permits optional additions, and documents tolerant handling of new output enum values. | Additive runtime releases must not break existing clients. |
| IS-114 = IDEAL-114 | The binary ships a version-matched `--skill` manual and schema, and every supported command and failure kind is discoverable without reading source. | An agent must not depend on hidden flags or stale external instructions. |
| IS-115 = IDEAL-115 | Structured logs correlate request, operation, principal, machine, runtime, resource, sequence, and outcome without mixing diagnostics into protocol stdout. | Cross-machine failures need traceable evidence rather than attach-text parsing. |
| IS-116 = IDEAL-116 | Health metrics expose active streams, per-consumer queue bytes, event lag, dropped/resynced ranges, RPC latency, and recovery state through read-only queries. | Operators need to distinguish consumer backpressure from runtime failure. |
| IS-117 = IDEAL-117 | Operation inspection distinguishes accepted, running, committed, failed, cancelled, and outcome-unknown states and exposes supporting receipts or process evidence. | A success-shaped message must never be the only proof that work finished. |
| GAP-001..019 | Connection, discovery, generation, correlation, strict targeting, deadlines, cancellation, idempotency, typed errors, safe status, schema compatibility. | Todos 2-4, 9, 13-15, 19-22, 29; part B finishes platform identity and security gates. |
| GAP-020..037 | Atomic snapshot/event boundaries, subscriptions, replay, loss, complete roster, stable lifecycle and completion facts. | Todos 9-10, 18, 22; part B supplies agent completion records and measured latency. |
| GAP-038..069 | PTY launch, attach readiness, observers, leases, first paint, platform parity, read fidelity, geometry, flow control and distinct incarnations. | Todos 5-7, 12, 16-17, 23-26, 30-32; part B proves Windows and restart parity. |
| GAP-070..086 | Atomic ordered input, receipts, prompt safety, cursor waits, interrupts, first-message readiness and paste encoding. | Todos 25, 27, 30, 32; part B supplies prompts, first-message orchestration and agent turn evidence. |
| GAP-087..105 | Agent identity, authoritative status, ownership, sessions, conversation records, histories and branches. | Todo 16 supplies process identity; part B's agent-domain todos close the structured-agent gaps. |
| GAP-106..120 | Stable machines, trust, outage isolation, layout, worktrees, templates, plugins and remote orphan propagation. | Todos 9, 13, 18 supply identity/layout foundations; part B closes federation and service gaps. |
| GAP-121..128 | Holder survival, restart, recovery exactness, reboot restoration, quarantine, lifecycle receipts, update and retention. | Todos 5-6, 8, 15-16 supply ownership/durability; part B closes recovery, update and retention end to end. |
| GAP-129..136 | Principal/scope enforcement, observe roles, revocation, report authority, separate takeover/answer rights, input validation, redaction and plugin scope. | Todos 11, 19, 24-25, 28 supply enforcement; part B closes agent, remote, plugin and retained-data security. |
| GAP-137..144 | Stable JSON CLI, safe discovery, one schema, compatibility, discoverability, correlated logs, metrics and inspectable operations. | Todos 3-4, 11, 15, 20-21, 29; part B completes embedded manual, metrics and release checks. |
| GAP-145..150 | Remove runtime chrome, brand manifests, transient failure presentation, discarded input, foreground promotion and path-derived split IDs. | Todos 1, 15, 18, 20, 25, 32; part B proves arbitrary-brand registration and durable agent outcomes. |

Every GAP id is closed by a todo in part A or part B. The ranges above are themes, not substitute Closes entries: every owning todo must enumerate individual IDs. Part A's ownership is listed in its todos; the complementary part B ownership is required before the assembled plan is executable. A prerequisite's Delivers row describes its observable contract slice; a property is globally accepted only after all platform/domain predicates and its end-to-end conformance gate pass.

### Must have

- Deliver all IDEAL-001..117 and close GAP-001..150 across the complete dependency-derived 20-wave plan; preserve the five already-good legacy contracts IDEAL-003, IDEAL-005, IDEAL-006, IDEAL-026 and IDEAL-098 as new implementation work.
- Rust edition 2024 Cargo workspace under `crates/`, Tokio bounded actors, serde/schemars protocol types, thiserror boundary errors, rusqlite with bundled SQLite, WAL and atomic state/receipt/event commits. Use a pure-Rust VT engine; select alacritty_terminal behind ovis-terminal-vt, never bare vte or ghostty-vt. Use portable-pty behind the frozen holder protocol, with a native Linux/macOS spike and native Windows ConPTY CI smoke.
- Keep these crate names: ovis-terminal-protocol, ovis-terminal-store, ovis-terminal-vt, ovis-terminal-holder, ovis-terminal-core, ovis-terminal-agent, ovis-terminal-agent-adapters, ovis-terminal-services, ovis-terminal-remote, ovis-terminal-authz, ovis-terminal-server and ovis-terminal-cli. The binary is `ovis-terminal`; `packages/client-ts/` contains the OpenRPC-generated TypeScript types and Bun client, with `conformance/`, `bench/`, `probes/`, `docs/` and GitHub Actions supporting it.
- Process model B+D: one replaceable control core plus one shared holder per session/build, old holders drain, and a documented dedicated-holder launch option remains an internal design option, not a public schema field in v1. Holders own PTYs/raw retained output, not VT parsing, layout or policy; the core owns VT checkpoints, layout, leases, operation state and domain events.
- A thin deterministic workspace/tab/pane/split-node layout service in core, including group-close scope and separate create/launch outcomes; no runtime presentation chrome. Stable machine/runtime/boot/pane/terminal/process/stream/lease identities must not be conflated.
- Linux and macOS first-class full functional QA; Windows contract-complete named pipes, ACLs, ConPTY, direct byte streams and logout-independent holders, with CI smoke as the platform proof rather than a claim of full Windows soak coverage.
- MIT LICENSE plus Apache-2.0 attribution and notices for herdr/herdr-core designs or code mirrored; track borrowed sources in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md. GitHub repository devswha/ovis-terminal is public, created by todo 1 during implementation, not during this drafting task.
- Deliver persistent multiplexed RPC, bounded event replay and streams, explicit leases, atomic ordered input, structured declared status/prompts/conversations, federation, services, recovery, schema/manual and observable operations. Ship a client contract for a later Ovis PR without changing the Ovis repository.

- Audience: Ovis maintainers, coding agents and producer authors; maintainer/operator and producer onboarding journeys are required in part B alongside the four binding user groups above. No new paid services, paid runners, signing purchases or provider API calls are authorized; use existing local/CI capacity and synthetic fixtures.
- Scale: keep the exact 16-PTY/eight-client/16-concurrent-request/1 MiB-per-second local latency profile and the maximum 100 ms verdict; 50-terminal capacity and 72-hour Linux/macOS soak are separate reported targets. Regulatory compliance: none stated; all security, privacy, retention and license predicates still apply.
- Support is limited to explicitly exercised OS/version/architecture artifacts, recorded by CI; native macOS builds and tests are required, with no Linux-to-macOS cross-compile claim. Signing/notarization status is documented rather than assumed.
- Provider matrix v1: authorized native stores already read by Ovis for claude, codex, pi/omo and opencode, plus one arbitrary unintegrated agent. Adapters emit conversation records only and NEVER lifecycle status; only declared OSC 7501/explicit owning-process reports have status authority. Missing supported-provider contracts cannot all be declared unavailable to claim closure.

### Must NOT have (guardrails, anti-slop, scope boundaries)

- **EXCL-001 - Runtime-owned sidebar, onboarding, and modal chrome.** Presentation belongs to clients and must not gate headless operations. (CAT-2380)
- **EXCL-002 - Prefix-key modal routing.** Runtime transport must not swallow bytes because an invisible Prefix, Navigate, Resize, or Copy mode is active. (CAT-968)
- **EXCL-003 - Mouse-only context-menu discovery.** Every operation must have a discoverable typed equivalent independent of pointer hit targets. (CAT-912)
- **EXCL-004 - Promoting a client to foreground on ordinary commands.** An RPC must not silently take the human's presentation or geometry authority. (CAT-2125)
- **EXCL-005 - Split targeting the focused pane.** Explicit target validation replaces mutation against whichever workspace happens to be focused. (CAT-2457, RT-HD-03)
- **EXCL-006 - Wrong-field process query falling back to focus.** A misspelled target must return an error rather than truthful data about the wrong pane. (RT-HD-02)
- **EXCL-007 - Automatic or implicit forced attach takeover.** Only an explicit authorized ownership transition may displace another controller. (RT-HD-07)
- **EXCL-008 - Screen-tail status guessing.** Screen text is terminal content, not authority for agent lifecycle. (CAT-1307, RT-HD-09)
- **EXCL-009 - Brand-detection manifests and their update machinery.** Agent registration must not require a runtime release or downloaded screen-matching rules. (CAT-688)
- **EXCL-010 - Unknown-brand agent lookup failure.** A declared agent must remain queryable regardless of brand, and a plain shell has an explicit null agent. (RT-HD-04, RT-HD-10)
- **EXCL-011 - Runtime toast timing, placement, and delivery policy.** Durable structured events replace transient runtime notification presentation. (CAT-1257)
- **EXCL-012 - Done and request sounds.** Ovis and terminal clients decide how to notify humans from factual events. (CAT-976)
- **EXCL-013 - Release notes and product-announcement modals.** Product messaging must not capture terminal input or become runtime session state. (CAT-535)
- **EXCL-014 - One-request sockets as the server contract.** A client may close voluntarily, but the server must support persistent multiplexed requests. (RT-HD-01)
- **EXCL-015 - Second subscription disabling delivery.** Filter updates must be acknowledged and preserve ongoing event delivery. (RT-HD-08)
- **EXCL-016 - Attach refusals and handoff reasons printed as terminal data.** Typed control outcomes replace misleading `server shut down` text while the server is alive. (RT-HD-07)
- **EXCL-017 - Undocumented fallback that discards unknown input sequences.** Explicit raw-byte transport and typed encoding errors replace silent consumption. (CAT-2586)
- **EXCL-018 - Hidden group-close flags.** Destructive scope must be visible in help, schema, validation, and receipts. (CAT-725)
- **EXCL-019 - Reading history by injecting scroll input and stitching screens.** Reads must not alter or suspend the application they inspect. (CAT-3161)
- **EXCL-020 - Detached custom commands with discarded output.** Every managed invocation needs an operation identity, bounded output, and an exit outcome. (CAT-472)
- **EXCL-021 - Errors expiring as a five-second banner.** Failure state must be queryable independently of whether a person saw a notification. (CAT-1039)
- **EXCL-022 - Split identity derived from a mutable tree path.** Stable node IDs prevent layout edits from retargeting an existing handle. (CAT-2520)
- **EXCL-023 - Blanket force-send bypass for blocked programs.** Versioned prompt answers or explicit scoped raw interaction replace an ordinary send overriding a blocker. (CAT-3290)
- **EXCL-024 - Send waiting five seconds for a status transition.** Admission, delivery, and later turn waits are separate operations. (RT-HC-01)
- **EXCL-025 - Output wait watching only bytes after registration.** Cursor-based or atomically armed waits preserve output produced during send. (RT-HC-02)
- **EXCL-026 - Interrupt reporting stopped from status alone.** Delivered interrupt and evidenced process or agent termination must remain separate facts. (RT-HC-03)
- **EXCL-027 - Read-only server status auto-starting a core.** Service creation is an explicit mutation, never a side effect of inspection. (RT-HC-04)

- No quiet-output readiness: first messages release only on declared readiness, never output silence. Provider-store ingestion is conversation-only and cannot become lifecycle authority.
- No edits to /home/devswha/workspace/ovis. Ovis paths in research are evidence only. No runtime TUI, screen-text lifecycle/prompt heuristics, brand detection, downloaded detection manifests, ghostty-vt, Zig, or Bun-hosted core/holder; the approved Rust decision overrides stale draft/advisory recommendations.
- No production unwrap/expect, including the invariant exception suggested by the general Rust README: the user's stricter rule wins. Use scoped `#[expect(lint, reason = "...")]`, never `#[allow]`. Use one thiserror enum per boundary with stable kind and hint, preserve source errors, and never turn failure into Null/default/success (PROG-IRON-unwrap, PROG-IRON-allow, PROG-IRON-error, RUST-HR-1; V-010, V-058, V-005).
- No numeric `as` casts: use checked `try_from` for fallible narrowing and `From` for proven widening, including explicit overflow policy. Enforce 250 pure-LOC per source file and at most three parameters per function with `scripts/check.sh` and its syntax-aware checker, including checked-in generated source; split by responsibility, not numbered fragments (PROG-IRON-cast, SLOP-10, PROG-SMELL-2; V-019, V-003, V-043).
- Rule totals also require typed newtypes and closed internal enums, parse-once boundaries, exhaustive matches, narrow explicit imports, no boolean mode flags, dead code, speculative pass-through abstractions, duplicated constants, swallowed Results or ambiguous error causes. Borrow existing library mechanisms before inventing parsers; paths stay PathBuf/OsStr; public fallible APIs document errors (PROG-A0..A6, SLOP-1..7, RUST-API-naming/conversions).
- Bound every mailbox, queue, retention window and allocation; move blocking SQLite/PTY work off Tokio workers, use cancellation tokens plus joined task trees and readiness signals, never sleep-to-synchronize tests or hold guards across await. Handle poison explicitly, document atomic ordering, and isolate unavoidable unsafe with a safe wrapper, SAFETY explanation, targeted Miri/model evidence and native OS tests (RUST-ASYNC-*, RUST-CONC-*, RUST-HR-2/3/5/6/7/8/9; V-215, V-220).
- One tracing pipeline logs once at the handling boundary with identity fields and redaction; no protocol stdout diagnostics, secret input logging, transient error banners or discarded invocation output (LOG-*, V-235). Workspace lint/toolchain/dependency gates are mandatory (RUST-CARGO-*); no tests disabled, retries used to hide flakiness, or prose snapshots that pin wording rather than machine-consumed values.

## Verification strategy
> Evidence root: every command below writes under `$ATTEMPT_DIR`, which the executor sets to `<repo>/.omo/evidence/ulw/<session>/<goalId>/a<attempt>` (ulw-loop currentAttemptDir) or `<repo>/.omo/evidence/<plan>/task-N/` outside a loop; both spellings in this plan refer to that one directory.

**Test decision.** TDD is mandatory for protocol/wire, store and VT: failing behavior test first, implementation second, proptest for fragmentation, round trips and state transitions, and insta for small reviewed structured records/checkpoints. Tests-after is allowed for mechanical CLI glue, but parse-before-connect and channel/exit-code behavior still need process tests; do not snapshot prose/help wording. Commit minimized proptest regressions; fix the cause of nondeterminism rather than repeating a green attempt.

The future executor runs from /home/devswha/workspace/ovis-terminal. Before every local Cargo invocation, initialize the shell with:

```sh
export RUSTUP_HOME=/tmp/ulw-ovis-yCJzPH/toolchain/rustup
export CARGO_HOME=/tmp/ulw-ovis-yCJzPH/toolchain/cargo
export PATH=/tmp/ulw-ovis-yCJzPH/toolchain/cargo/bin:$PATH
```

CI installs the declared toolchains rather than using these workstation paths. `scripts/check.sh` runs nightly rustfmt check, stable Clippy over all workspace targets/features with `-D warnings`, `cargo nextest run --workspace --all-targets --all-features`, doctests, and pure-LOC/parameter checks. Cargo deny, rustdoc warnings, MSRV, dependency hygiene and any unsafe-specific Miri gate run in CI. A test filter is successful only if its named test actually ran; zero-test exit 0 is a failure of evidence.

**Real surface.** Todo 21 builds `cargo run --locked --manifest-path conformance/Cargo.toml --bin conformance -- --binary target/debug/ovis-terminal --scenario <family/name> --isolated --case <happy|failure|all> --evidence "$ATTEMPT_DIR/task-N-ovis-terminal-v1.json"`. It launches the actual `ovis-terminal` binary in a fresh isolated XDG/session root, awaits an endpoint-ready signal, speaks the real Unix socket/named pipe/WebSocket protocol, checks typed records and PTY bytes, and cleans up only its owned processes. Scenario IDs named below are implementation requirements, not existing tools. Each scenario stores requests, replies, ordered stream offsets, process identities, stdout/stderr, exit status and measured bounds; it exits 0 only when the expected success or expected typed rejection was observed, and exits 1 on a mismatch, timeout, missing test or missing evidence. Foundational integration tests use real local sockets/PTY fixtures directly before the runner exists; later conformance reruns the contract through the final binary.

No fixed sleeps: register a channel/event/PTY marker before triggering actions; use bounded awaits and paused virtual time for deadlines, heartbeats and expiry. Process fixtures can explicitly withhold reads/acks, release barriers, signal EOF and expose write boundaries. Fault injection happens at named commit/admission/write/ack barriers, including cancelled and resumed runs, repeated interruption, stale epoch, malformed data, permission revocation and misleading success. Prompt-like bytes are hostile data, never authority; no paid/live-provider account is required. Dirty-worktree tests use disposable repositories only. Planning-time validation parses every literal JSON --inject payload; schema/type rejection (invalid_params) is checked separately from semantically stale canonical values and their domain errors.

Evidence root: `/home/devswha/workspace/ovis-terminal/.omo/evidence/ulw/<session>/<goalId>/a<attempt>`. The executor sets `attemptDir` to that concrete directory; every todo writes `<attemptDir>/task-N-ovis-terminal-v1.<ext>` and its PR pastes exact before/after evidence with a transcript reference, not just a test-count claim. PR CI URLs and commit IDs accompany local evidence. Platform-qualified results must name OS/arch/toolchain; no Linux pass is Windows proof.

| CI lane | Mandatory checks and observable evidence |
|---|---|
| ubuntu-latest | Full fmt/Clippy/nextest/doctest/docs/deny/MSRV/checker gates, Unix socket and real PTY conformance, store crash barriers, protocol/property tests, generated-client checks, full Linux functional QA. |
| macos-latest | Same shared tests and real PTY/socket conformance, native process start tokens, resize/first paint, holder detach/logout and crash/restart functional QA; capture short socket-path limits. |
| windows-latest | Build/Clippy/shared tests and CI smoke proving named-pipe ACL/identity, ConPTY spawn, direct attach/modes/resize/ack, input ordering and detached-holder survival; no mirroring fallback or unsupported-success stub. |
| Linux supplemental | Miri on pure unsafe wrappers where supported, native tests for OS FFI, bounded loom/scheduler interleavings where applicable, schema compatibility against released baseline, SDK regeneration/typecheck/Bun tests, release/notice audit. |
| Performance/soak | Dedicated Linux/macOS runs with at most 16 PTYs, eight clients, 16 simultaneous control requests and 1 MiB/s output for the 100 ms admission/event contracts; 50-PTY stress/72-hour soak is separate from the local acceptance profile. Windows remains CI smoke only. |

**Conformance coverage.** Map each IDEAL and GAP to a scenario and owning todo; never mark a prerequisite as full platform acceptance. Test at least 64 MiB logical reads through frames no larger than 16 MiB, slow consumers with no final writable error frame, whole-snapshot pagination at one revision, same-generation additive enums, reboot-versus-restart identity, and no auto-resend after unknown delivery. Final part B gates close the full 117/150 coverage ledger, license/schema freshness, security, platform and release obligations.

## Execution strategy

Use the approved draft's approval outcome and current user decisions, not its superseded Bun/VT suggestions. Architect section 5 W1/W2/W3 supplies the responsibilities; Rust crate boundaries replace its package names, W3-G becomes packages/client-ts, and its Ovis cutover wave becomes in-repository contract/handoff fixtures. The complete ultrabrain advisory's terminals-only layout exclusion is overruled by IDEAL-084..088; its per-terminal Bun holder, raw upstream input and optional human-lease bypass are not adopted. The complete ultrabrain-spec-full.md supersedes the elided file for method names, state machines, per-RT fixes and edge cases; consultant finding 1 is resolved by that recovered source. Binding scope and the steering decisions below override its remaining contradictions.

**Settled contracts.** Persistent NDJSON is control-only; a successful hello can upgrade to a nine-byte `[channel:u32 LE][kind:u8][len:u32 LE]` framed connection at an explicit parser boundary. Channel 0 carries JSON-RPC; DATA on nonzero channels is downstream terminal data, CREDIT contains an eight-byte little-endian cumulative acknowledged filtered-stream delivery offset bound to its never-reused channel and stream identity, and CLOSE is typed control; input/resize/focus/takeover are RPCs, never anonymous stream writes. Oversize/truncated/unknown frames are typed errors, not ignored; use 16 MiB frames, 16 KiB output chunks, 256 KiB initial credit, 1 MiB maximum outstanding credit, 4 MiB per-stream backlog, 16 MiB input queue, 10,000 events/64 MiB replay, 15-second idle heartbeat and 10-second ordinary deadline as advertised reversible defaults. JSON u64 counters/offsets/revisions use canonical decimal strings so generated JavaScript cannot lose precision. Raw holder output offsets are separate from filtered stream delivery offsets, which include checkpoint bytes; attach control records bind both domains, and CREDIT acknowledges only the latter. Filter VT-answered queries from downstream bytes while retaining raw output for recovery, and suppress query replies/status side effects during checkpoint replay.

**Consistency.** One terminal actor serializes admission and input barriers; the store's writer allocates the runtime-lifetime sequence and atomically commits state, operation receipts and events. Reads use a stable WAL snapshot, and publication follows commit. Control restart changes server_boot_id, not runtime_lifetime while holders survive; reboot starts a new lifetime and reports restored metadata. Snapshot pagination pins one revision. Input authority, target/process/lease identity and mode revision are checked at admission and again at the actual queued-write boundary; takeover/revocation fences unsent entries, without claiming to retract bytes already written. No human-controller bypass is implicit; whenever any lease exists, writes require the current interact lease, and lease-free agent writes require an explicitly granted input scope. Terminal-query replies use a distinct internal bounded lane and cannot become user submissions or be answered twice by viewers.

**Worker sizing and ownership.** Todo 1 is the serial scaffold gate and its PR is the only lane allowed to land before any crate compiles; it must leave the entire workspace compiling before any later lane lands. It creates every manifest and lint-inheriting crate root, tooling package, SDK package manifest, dependency seam, module declaration and typed dispatch registration slot needed by the declared lanes. Every later lane edits existing scaffolds, adds its owned implementation/tests and must be independently buildable and green against the landed scaffold, without waiting for a dependent assembly lane to supply its package or route. Unimplemented methods are not advertised or registered as success-shaped placeholders. At most eight workers run concurrently, and same-wave write scopes must remain disjoint; root/manifest changes stay with their named owner. Every implementation todo includes its tests and one verified conventional commit.


**Steering and complete-spec reconciliation.** Use the method names from ultrabrain-spec-full.md: attach.open/resize/release_geometry/repaint/credit/close/takeover/leases, terminal.wait_output, events.wait, input.send/keys/raw/paste/interrupt/barrier, op.get/cancel and agent.conversation/history. Add explicitly typed layout, selection, worktree, plugin and update methods missing from its 81-method catalog; todo 4 owns the complete versioned catalog rather than treating the 81 count as full coverage. All 25 edge cases and all 20 RT observations become traceability scenarios, with resize/focus represented as RPC, observer DATA rejected immediately, write-boundary mode revalidation, explicit-stop evidence and reboot proof overriding the conflicting advisory examples.

**Thread and durability model.** Tokio uses the multithread runtime with one serialized terminal actor and bounded message channels per terminal. Blocking portable-pty pumps and SQLite calls run off the async executor using bounded spawn_blocking admission or dedicated long-lived pump/DB threads; every task is cancellable and joined, and VT work yields to measured budgets. Pin portable-pty, alacritty_terminal, rusqlite/bundled SQLite and the C compiler/linker version per native CI target; bundled SQLite is C, not a pure-Rust database. The pure-Rust requirement concerns the VT engine. Recovery evidence persists across reads; neither server.status nor server.metrics clears it. Preserve DB/WAL and unsupported-schema material, require independent OS boot and holder/process incarnation proof, and never automatically relaunch while survival remains inconclusive.

**Aggregate bounds.** Advertise both per-object and session-wide limits. Initial configurable targets are 1 GiB total runtime-plus-holder RSS and 4 GiB retained session data including output rings, checkpoints/history and database/WAL, excluding evidence storage; reserve 64 MiB aggregate for accepted input queues, 64 MiB shared output backlog references, 64 MiB event queues, 128 MiB VT resident grids/history and the remainder for measured process/library overhead. Limits are admission budgets, not preallocations; the lower session budget wins, disk-backed history advances visible floors, and bounded reserved control capacity remains available. At 50 terminals, 64 MiB raw rings total 3.125 GiB and a single shared payload store avoids 400 separate 4 MiB copies; fixture stress must prove measured RSS/disk stays within negotiated caps or fail the gate. No benchmark may silently loosen the budgets.

**Windows release gate.** Part B's native Windows todo must implement owner-DACL named pipes, principal authentication, first-instance ownership and concurrent accept/reconnect, portable-pty ConPTY and holder isolation from SSH/job teardown. Smoke covers actual spawn, two attach clients, input, resize, mode/cursor parity, final-output-before-exit, observer denial and core restart with unchanged process incarnation, then controlling-job exit with surviving holder; no 72-hour Windows soak is required or claimed.

**Journeys and traceability.** Todo 21 creates docs/design/traceability.json with every IDEAL/GAP/EXCL mapped to owning todo and scenario, including part B owners; validation fails missing, duplicate-ID, unknown or unimplemented scenario references at release. Part B must prove GAP-099 through provider-store discovery with an authorized opaque store identity and provenance, not guessed cwd/recency; ship producer registration/authenticated-report/prompt-answer/history examples and operator install/remove/service/rollback/corruption/disk-full diagnosis journeys. Consumer fixtures include stale prompt cards, uncertain-send drafts, credential expiry, explicit takeover/trust consent and cross-account denial without editing Ovis. Rust and the Bun SDK replay the same language-neutral wire fixtures.

**PR baseline rule.** Todo 1 copies redacted original smoke transcripts into docs/evidence/baseline/ with source path, line number and digest. Before is a cited original transcript line (plus durable copy) or the literal word absent; absent is not an executed failing test and a legacy keeper is not a defect. After is the actual ovis-terminal CLI/socket invocation plus parsed result/bytes, exit status and OS/architecture; unit test exit alone is insufficient. Foundational component commits can land behind tests, but their feature PR handoff must wait for the corresponding real-binary conformance result after assembly. No plan text claims those future commands already ran.

### Parallel execution waves
> Waves are topological levels of the Blocked-by graph: every dependency points to an earlier wave. A wave with fewer than three todos exists only where real dependencies force it.
- Wave 1: 1 (Bootstrap the public repository and strict workspace)
- Wave 2: 2 (Implement incremental channel framing and bounded decoding); 3 (Define correlated RPC envelopes and boundary error types); 5 (Define holder protocol and adoption identity contracts); 7 (Build the pure Rust VT state engine); 8 (Implement atomic SQLite storage and quarantine primitives); 11 (Add structured tracing and secret redaction foundations)
- Wave 3: 4 (Generate OpenRPC and enforce released schema compatibility); 6 (Implement Unix PTY holders and bounded pumps); 12 (Establish native platform probes and performance harnesses)
- Wave 4: 9 (Define identity resolution and lifecycle transition tables)
- Wave 5: 10 (Coordinate commits snapshots and subscription barriers); 19 (Enforce principals roles and resource scoped authority)
- Wave 6: 13 (Serve authenticated persistent endpoints and negotiated hello); 15 (Persist operation deduplication and inspectable outcomes)
- Wave 7: 14 (Dispatch strict targets with deadlines and cancellation); 16 (Integrate terminal lifecycle and process incarnation evidence)

- Wave 9: 21 (Build real socket conformance runner and protocol fixtures); 32 (Encode literal text keys and paste without hidden execution)
- Wave 10: 22 (Implement independent subscriptions replay and recoverable loss); 25 (Fence ordered input at admission and actual writes); 33 (Register agents and preserve authoritative OSC status); 43 (Authenticate stable machines over byte transparent transports); 45 (Manage explicit worktree identities and destructive scope); 46 (Persist scoped plugin catalogs and bounded run outcomes); 50 (Commit versioned documents and visible retention floors); 51 (Separate Unix service lifetime and endpoint discovery)
- Wave 11: 23 (Serve typed attach outcomes and isolated output streams); 27 (Implement atomic cursor waits and evidenced interrupt outcomes); 36 (Bind sessions and version conversation history cursors); 53 (Own Windows PTYs through safe ConPTY boundaries)
- Wave 12: 26 (Order geometry transitions and unchanged grid repaint); 28 (Expose authenticated WebSocket transport with parity); 34 (Commit versioned prompts and independently authorize answers); 35 (Launch versioned templates with readiness bound messages); 37 (Expose typed conversation blocks and turn records); 48 (Preserve program incarnations during planned control restart); 54 (Serve named pipes with Windows service isolation); 55 (Report process incarnations consistently across operating systems); 56 (Bound VT scheduling and preserve control latency)
- Wave 13: 29 (Generate TypeScript SDK and replay neutral fixtures); 31 (Assemble the binary and plain interactive attach client); 38 (Parse authorized Claude and Codex native stores); 40 (Bridge typed in process prompt widgets); 41 (Track parent incarnations and child lifecycle policy); 47 (Stage verified updates with inspectable rollback outcomes); 49 (Recover holders checkpoints and honest reboot metadata); 57 (Rotate credentials and recheck queued mutation authority)
- Wave 14: 30 (Prove attach input and aggregate capacity conformance); 39 (Adapt Pi OmO and OpenCode conversation stores); 44 (Isolate remote actors and replay orphan acknowledgements); 58 (Enforce scoped paths payloads and diagnostic redaction); 59 (Expose read only health and per consumer metrics); 62 (Attach an interactive terminal without multiplexer chrome)
- Wave 15: 42 (Coordinate durable commits snapshots and agent assembly); 52 (Exercise remote services and recovery over real endpoints)
- Wave 16: 60 (Run first class Unix QA and Windows smoke CI); 61 (Enforce Rust quality and violation checklist gates)
- Wave 17: 63 (Package generated TypeScript types and persistent Bun client); 69 (Benchmark fifty terminals against published latency budgets)
- Wave 18: 64 (Ship replayable Ovis consumer conformance fixtures); 66 (Assemble the single binary and embedded producer manual); 70 (Ship the 72-hour soak harness and prove a 30-minute continuous run)
- Wave 19: 65 (Document every Ovis adapter migration boundary); 67 (Build verified cross platform release artifacts)
- Wave 20: 68 (Install releases into explicit isolated directories)

- [ ] 24. Implement independent input and geometry lease ownership
  What to do / Must NOT do: Implement concurrent observe attachments with no input/grid side effects and independent interact and geometry lease IDs/epochs. Disconnect/release suspends or revokes authority deterministically and transfers geometry only among eligible interact clients using a published stable acquisition-order rule, never browser focus. Whenever any lease exists on a terminal, all user/agent writes require the current interact lease; address possession alone never grants authority. ### Scope and fixed contracts

Audience: Ovis maintainers, coding agents, producer/adapter/plugin authors, runtime operators, terminal/phone humans and shared-machine account owners. Budget: no new paid services, purchases, signing services or provider API usage; all agent/provider scenarios use synthetic local fixtures and existing local/CI capacity. Regulatory compliance: none stated; IDEAL-102..109, MIT licensing and Apache-2.0 attribution still apply. Ovis at /home/devswha/workspace/ovis is READ-ONLY; this plan supplies its client contract, fixtures and migration guide, never its cutover.

Runtime is a Rust edition-2024 Cargo workspace with the named crates under crates/. Use alacritty_terminal for the grid/state engine, never bare vte as a substitute, ghostty-vt, Zig, @xterm/headless or Bun runtime services. PTYs use pinned portable-pty behind a frozen holder protocol; Todo 12 must prove it on native Linux/macOS before dependent lanes, and native Windows smoke proves its ConPTY path. Use rusqlite bundled with pinned SQLite C/compiler/linker versions, WAL and a dedicated bounded DB writer. Tokio is multi-threaded with bounded per-terminal actor channels, bounded spawn_blocking submissions for blocking PTY/DB boundaries, explicitly owned long-lived pump/DB threads and joined shutdown; no task is an OS security sandbox. Rust tooling lives under conformance/, bench/ and probes/ as workspace test/benchmark binaries; Bun is used ONLY inside packages/client-ts for the generated SDK and Ovis-side tests.

One shared holder process runs per session per build. A documented internal dedicated-holder launch option is available to isolation/fault tests but is absent from the public v1 OpenRPC schema and CLI flags. Holders own bytes, PTYs, bounded raw tails and exit facts; the CORE owns all VT parsing, query answering and offset-fenced checkpoints. lifetime_id survives a core crash/restart; server_boot_id changes; terminal/process/stream incarnations are distinct. Todo 48 owns the tested transition table, and 49 requires independent boot/incarnation evidence before restoration or relaunch; ambiguous survival never causes a duplicate launch. Recovery evidence is durable and reads never clear it.

Wire is persistent NDJSON JSON-RPC plus negotiated nine-byte framed mode on the SAME connection. Channel zero carries correlated control; terminal channels are download-only, with CREDIT/CLOSE upstream; input, resize and client-labelled selection/focus are RPCs. The core answers VT queries once and removes answered queries from attach bytes, exposing raw output_offset and separate delivered stream offsets/credit so removed bytes cannot fabricate credit. Inputs are closed/strict; output enum additions are tolerated. Pagination carries total, continuation, truncation and a pinned snapshot revision for logical results >=64 MiB. Todo 42 owns commit-sequence allocation, publication order, snapshot pagination and subscription-install barriers. The runtime retains thin deterministic workspaces/tabs/panes/splits and selection events identifying the client; ordinary structural work cannot steal focus.

Whenever any interact lease exists, every write requires its current lease (or an explicitly granted delegation bound to it), with process/session/grant fencing at the holder write boundary, including queued writes. Immediate sends are ADMISSION receipts only; PTY delivery, agent acceptance and turn facts arrive later through receipt updates/op inspection. Literal text is encoded independently of submit; unsafe multiline/control bytes are rejected when negotiated mode cannot preserve them, even with submit:none. Agent readiness for the queued FIRST launch message is declared only, never quiet output; runtime stream-geometry readiness after resize is asserted by the runtime after VT apply and holder acknowledgement (Todo 26), without a producer report. Provider adapters emit conversation records only and have no lifecycle-status authority; OSC 7501 and authenticated explicit producer reports are authoritative. Prompt mechanisms and choices must be explicitly declared: do not infer a synthetic answer mechanism from blocked screen text or arbitrary OSC message prose.

Use the full specification's names where they do not conflict: attach.open/resize/repaint/takeover/leases, input.send/keys/raw/paste/barrier/interrupt, status.report/clear/get, prompt.open/get/list/answer/close, agent.register/update_session/conversation/history/children/note/wait_children, template.list/get, doc.*, machine.*, rpc.discover and schema.*. Layout, worktree, plugin and staged local update methods are explicit additions owned by their protocol leaves. A stale status sequence is the typed stale_seq error with current_seq, not a success-shaped superseded acknowledgement. Internal orphan acknowledgement is not an unscoped public mutation. The complete source-of-truth schema and docs/runtime-contract.md record these overrides; the advisory's permissive literal text, raw stream DATA, synthetic prompts, quiet readiness and automatic inconclusive recovery are NOT contracts to copy.

The exact latency profile remains 16 PTYs, eight clients, 16 simultaneous control requests and 1 MiB/s aggregate output on writable local sockets; every admitted send and committed event must meet the 100 ms IDEAL bound, not only p99. The 50-terminal capacity/72-hour-soak target is separately reported on Linux/macOS only; the requested 50-terminal p99 <=100 ms is a separate release-capacity gate, not a rewritten IDEAL assumption. Initial aggregate session budgets: <=1 GiB total core/holder RSS and <=4 GiB retained session data (evidence storage excluded), with <=64 MiB aggregate accepted input, <=64 MiB aggregate stream backlog references, <=64 MiB event queues and shared immutable output chunks; ring/history quotas fit the disk budget, and quota pressure advances visible floors or rejects new work before allocation. Record the actual aggregate derivation and stress it in 56/69, rather than multiplying every per-object maximum. Windows has implemented identical required outcomes but only native CI smoke, not full manual QA or a Windows soak.

The release artifact matrix is Linux x86_64/aarch64 musl, macOS x86_64/aarch64 and Windows x86_64 MSVC. Linux uses pinned native/cross C toolchains; macOS builds on native macOS with Apple SDK/clang, never an unproven Linux-to-macOS build. Todo 60 records actual OS version, architecture, runner label, PTY/service/install results and permissions; only exercised pairs are supported, and an untested artifact is blocked, not silently advertised. Native macOS full QA and existing long-lived Linux/macOS soak capacity are release prerequisites; an unavailable existing runner is a reported blocker, never authorization to buy one. State signing/notarization as performed or not provided; do not claim untested versions.

### Execution and evidence contract

Commands below describe implementation deliverables, not checks already run by this drafting session. Run from /home/devswha/workspace/ovis-terminal. Before ANY local cargo command execute:

```sh
export RUSTUP_HOME=/tmp/ulw-ovis-yCJzPH/toolchain/rustup CARGO_HOME=/tmp/ulw-ovis-yCJzPH/toolchain/cargo PATH=/tmp/ulw-ovis-yCJzPH/toolchain/cargo/bin:$PATH
mkdir -p .omo/evidence/ovis-terminal-v1
export ATTEMPT_DIR="$(mktemp -d "$PWD/.omo/evidence/ovis-terminal-v1/attempt.XXXXXX")"
```

CI installs the pinned native Rust/C toolchains instead of these workstation paths. Each invocation of the Rust conformance runner creates a private session, XDG/HOME tree, sockets/pipes, credentials, repositories and synthetic processes; it subscribes to exact readiness/state events before actions, has bounded deadlines and cleans up only its own resources. It never edits Ovis or uses a user's agent store. The runner invocation is `cargo run --locked --manifest-path conformance/Cargo.toml --bin conformance -- --binary target/debug/ovis-terminal --scenario <family/name> --isolated --case <happy|failure|all> --evidence "$ATTEMPT_DIR/task-N-ovis-terminal-v1.json"`; its contract is introduced by Todo 21 and extended by each owning lane. `--case failure --inject '<JSON>'` merges the shown field override into that scenario's otherwise-valid request/identity bindings; `principal_fixture`, `fixture` and `fixture:*` values select test setup, not wire fields. The emitted evidence MUST include the fully materialized JSON-RPC/bytes and exact binary CLI/socket command so no placeholder is treated as a sent request. Non-JSON injections select the literal CLI invocation shown. Expected typed errors make a negative case pass; unexpected success, wrong field/error, no matching test, missing method or absent assertion fails the runner. Every scenario named below is implemented with its feature, not left as a later placeholder.

Each lane runs its unit/property tests and the quoted real-binary scenario; the runner checks both the happy and negative assertions and writes one combined evidence object plus raw companions. No fixed sleeps, polling-to-green, automatic test retries or prose-pinning tests. Fixture clocks/actor barriers make timing-independent races deterministic; time is measured only for latency, heartbeat and soak behavior. Shared crate entry points, Cargo manifests and typed dispatch slots are created by Todo 1 so leaf owners can compile and exercise their real route without racing root edits; integration lanes own the final composition checks, not delayed feature tests. Todo 1 owns all required scaffold seams before dispatch. Cargo outputs and generated bulk files stay in target/ or dist/; only the releasing lane promotes the versioned schema baseline.

Part B contains exactly 38 numbered lanes (33-70), each one worker run including its tests. The dependency-derived waves above replace the old domain-wave groupings; all Blocked by edges cross strictly earlier waves, and same-wave write scopes remain disjoint. No ultrabrain lane is allocated in part B.

## Final verification wave

All six checks run after todos 1-70 have landed, on the same candidate SHA. Commands below are contracts for tools delivered by todos 21, 60, 61, 64, 69 and 70; they are not claims that those tools exist today. Initialize ATTEMPT_DIR and the local Cargo environment as specified above; CI uses its native toolchain. Every check exits 0 only with all required assertions satisfied, records binary/schema hashes and fails on missing evidence or skipped required scenarios. Implementation completion uses the continuous 1800-second proof from Todo 70; the 72-hour scheduled results remain explicitly deferred. The orchestrator keeps release completion blocked until both Linux and macOS have full evidence and continuous 259200-second soak receipts, then reruns the release evidence gates for the candidate SHA.
